 -->![](http://wizards.com//mtg/images/daily/events/gppar14/closing.jpg)  
  

Day One of Grand Prix Paris 2014 is in the books. And what a day it was! We saw amazing **Magic** being played by some of the world's best and brightest, and we saw the Legacy format at its best. 1,587 hopefuls entered the tournament but only 186 of them managed to escape the first nine rounds with scores of 7-2 or better. 

Among them, Hall of Famer Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa, Kasper Euser, Maxime Gilles, Philipp Schönegger, and Gregorio Soriente lead the field overnight with unblemished records of 9-0. Close behind in the standings, however, are players like Andreas Ganz, Elias Klocker, and Carlos Moral, all on 8-1, and 7-2s like Joel Larsson, Alexandre Darras, Pierre Dagen, (25) Christian Calcano, Marijn Lybaert, and Jan van der Vegt certainly can't be discounted either. It's going to be an interesting day tomorrow, when the players race to the finish line, when the Top 8 will be decided, and when, finally, a new champion will be crowned.

Join us then, both on text and video, as we bring you all the action, all the excitement, and all the tech straight from the battlefield. Until then, from Paris, we wish you a good night!

*   ### Saturday, 9:29 a.m. – The Legacy Format in 32 Sentences

    *by Tobi Henke*
  

With in excess of 20 years of **Magic** sets to pick from, the number of viable decks in Legacy is mind-boggling. Since there are so many, let's just get started, shall we? 

**RUG Delver/Canadian Threshold** – This long-time favorite runs 18 lands including four [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland)s which are hardly ever used for mana production, manages to get by with the help of [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) and [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder), adds further disruption in [Stifle](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stifle), [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze), and [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) plus a little firepower in [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), and kills with cheap threats like [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver+of+Secrets), [Nimble Mongoose](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nimble+Mongoose), and [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). 

**Stoneblade** – On the control side of the aggro-control spectrum, this deck features [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) for [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull), [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte), and usually one Sword of somethong-or-other along with [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) in white, a few counterspells, the ubiquitous [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm), [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), [Snapcaster Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapcaster+Mage), and [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis) as an equipment bearer in blue, and mosten often adds black for a bit of discard, sometimes [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls), or the odd [Vindicate](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vindicate). 

**BUG Delver** – Also called "Team America," the deck resembles its red-blue-green counterpart, but uses [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) instead of [Nimble Mongoose](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nimble+Mongoose) to go a little higher on the mana curve, enabling more potent disruption like [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach) and [Abrupt Decay](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abrupt+Decay) as well as stuff like [Tombstalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tombstalker) and [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil). 

**Storm** – This deck's game usually starts with a library manipulation and/or discard spell, next are mana rituals and artifacts like [Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond) and [Lotus Petal](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Petal), then comes [Infernal Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Infernal+Tutor) or [Burning Wish](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burning+Wish), [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) or [Past in Flames](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Past+in+Flames) to continue chaining spells, and finally there's [Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony) or [Empty the Warrens](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empty+the+Warrens) for the killing blow, which can be dealt as early as turn one! 

**Sneak and Show** – This deck's goal is to get a fast [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) or [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn) onto the battlefield via [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) and/or [Sneak Attack](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sneak+Attack), and it uses [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) and [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) to find its combo parts, some countermagic to protect them, and some combination of [Ancient Tomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Tomb), [Lotus Petal](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Petal), and [City of Traitors](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=City+of+Traitors) for additional speed. 

**Miracles** – One of the few true control decks of the format, this white-blue build includes a lot of the staples of the format like [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) and [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares), but also enlists, together with [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) and [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), the help of [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) to get full mileage out of [Terminus](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminus), [Entreat the Angels](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entreat+the+Angels), and [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance). 

**UWR Stoneblade/Delver** – Rather new to scene, this hybrid retains the low land count and spell focus of other [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver+of+Secrets) decks while adding a somewhat smaller Stoneblade package with just two equipments and two copies of [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis) along with four [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic)s, for a total of ten creatures. 

**Jund** – Based on the time-honored strategy of putting all the best green, red, and black cards into one deck, the list here includes [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman), [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), [Abrupt Decay](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abrupt+Decay), [Punishing Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Punishing+Fire) plus [Grove of the Burnwillows](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grove+of+the+Burnwillows), [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach), [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), and [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil). 

**Elves** – The deck quickly busts out a lot of Elves with particular help of [Gaea's Cradle](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaea%27s+Cradle) and the [Heritage Druid](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Heritage+Druid)/[Nettle Sentinel](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nettle+Sentinel) combination as well as the [Wirewood Symbiote](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wirewood+Symbiote)/[Elvish Visionary](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Elvish+Visionary) combination, can draw an insane amount of cards with [Glimpse of Nature](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glimpse+of+Nature) in one big combo turn, and/or kill quickly by searching up [Craterhoof Behemoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Craterhoof+Behemoth) with [Natural Order](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Natural+Order). 

**Goblins** – Here, [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=%C3%86ther+Vial) or [Goblin Lackey](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Lackey) lead the way for, among others, [Goblin Warchief](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Warchief), [Goblin Piledriver](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Piledriver), [Goblin Matron](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Matron), and [Goblin Ringleader](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ringleader), while [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) and [Rishadan Port](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rishadan+Port) disrupt the opponent's mana. 

**Merfolk** – The deck basically consists of a few counterspells, a set of [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=%C3%86ther+Vial)s, and a whole lof of fish like [Lord of Atlantis](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lord+of+Atlantis), [Master of the Pearl Trident](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+the+Pearl+Trident), and [Merrow Reejerey](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merrow+Reejerey) which pack quite a punch. 

**Death and Taxes** – A white weenie deck with [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) plus [Rishadan Port](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rishadan+Port), [Thalia, Guardian of Thraben](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thalia%2C+Guardian+of+Thraben), [Phyrexian Revoker](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phyrexian+Revoker), and [Aven Mindcensor](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Mindcensor) for disruption, [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=%C3%86ther+Vial), [Flickerwisp](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flickerwisp), and [Mother of Runes](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mother+of+Runes) for protection, and [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) and [Mirran Crusader](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mirran+Crusader) for the beats. 

**Imperial Painter** – [Ancient Tomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Tomb), [City of Traitors](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=City+of+Traitors), and [Simian Spirit Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Simian+Spirit+Guide) enable an explosive opening, [Blood Moon](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Moon), [Magus of the Moon](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Moon), and [Red Elemental Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Red+Elemental+Blast) offer disruption, [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) and [Imperial Recruiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Imperial+Recruiter) offer some card selection, and [Grindstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grindstone), in combination with [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant), dumps the opponent's library for the kill. 

**Maverick** – Traditionally a green-white beatdown deck with a creature toolbox searchable via [Green Sun's Zenith](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Green+Sun%27s+Zenith) and a land toolbox searchable via [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), nowadays Maverick almost invariably splashes red for the [Grove of the Burnwillows](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grove+of+the+Burnwillows)/[Punishing Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Punishing+Fire) combo. 

**Pox** – A mono-black deck that heavily attacks both the opponent's mana as well as hand with [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland), [Sinkhole](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sinkhole), [Smallpox](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smallpox), [Inquisition of Kozilek](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inquisition+of+Kozilek), [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach), and [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil), leaving a minimum of slots to provide the actual kill with [Mishra's Factory](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mishra%27s+Factory) and a couple of [Nether Spirit](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nether+Spirit)s and [Cursed Scroll](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cursed+Scroll). 

**Nic Fit** – A black-green-blue midrange deck with a lot of variety that combines [Cabal Therapy](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Therapy) and [Veteran Explorer](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Veteran+Explorer) to ramp into creatures larger than what one usually sees cast in Legacy. 

 **Omni-Tell** – This takes the shell of Sneak and Show (see above) but leaves out most of the creatures and [Sneak Attack](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sneak+Attack) in favor of [Omniscience](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Omniscience), [Dream Halls](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dream+Halls), and [Enter the Infinite](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Enter+the+Infinite). 

**Reanimator** – [Careful Study](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Study) or [Entomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entomb) bins a big creature like [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) for [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate) or [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) to get it onto the battlefield, while [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), and/or [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) offer disruption/protection. 

**Tin Fins** – A reanimator deck with an even faster kill, which reanimates [Griselbrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Griselbrand) via [Shallow Grave](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shallow+Grave) or [Goryo's Vengeance](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goryo%27s+Vengeance), draws a bunch of cards, attacks, draws more cards, summons [Children of Korlis](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Children+of+Korlis) to gain yet more life/extra cards, and finishes the opponent with mana artifacts/rituals and [Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony). 

**Dredge** – Playing mostly from the graveyard, this deck first needs to dump a dredge card like [Golgari Grave-Troll](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Grave-Troll) per [Careful Study](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Study), [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting), or [Putrid Imp](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Imp), then dredges and dredges more with [Breakthrough](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Breakthrough) or [Cephalid Coliseum](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cephalid+Coliseum), eventually getting a bunch of [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba)s and [Ichorid](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ichorid)s back from the grave, along with free copies of [Cabal Therapy](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Therapy) and [Dread Return](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread+Return), creating a Zombie army via [Bridge from Below](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bridge+from+Below). 

**Belcher** – Running as little as one [Taiga](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Taiga) as its only land, this deck instead uses [Elvish Spirit Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Elvish+Spirit+Guide), [Simian Spirit Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Simian+Spirit+Guide), mana artifacts like [Chrome Mox](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chrome+Mox) or [Lotus Petal](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Petal), and all kinds of mana rituals, from [Rite of Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rite+of+Flame) to [Pyretic Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyretic+Ritual), to either cast [Empty the Warrens](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empty+the+Warrens) or cast and activate the eponymous [Goblin Charbelcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Charbelcher), killing on turn one or two. 

**Allspells** – Similar to Belcher, here a player only needs four mana to cast/activate either [Balustrade Spy](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Balustrade+Spy) or [Undercity Informer](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Undercity+Informer), dumping all of their library, which in turn triggers [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba)s, enables the casting of [Cabal Therapy](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Therapy) and [Dread Return](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread+Return) to reanimte [Angel of Glory's Rise](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Glory%27s+Rise) and, with it, [Laboratory Maniac](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Laboratory+Maniac) plus [Azami, Lady of Scrolls](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azami%2C+Lady+of+Scrolls) to win the game. 

**High Tide** – This mono-blue engine combo deck generates a lot of mana with the help of its namesake card and untap spells like [Turnabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Turnabout) and—in different versions—*Time Spiral* or [Reset](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reset), draws a lot of cards with [Meditate](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meditate), and kills with [Brain Freeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brain+Freeze). 

**Lands** – There are a couple of different decks running [Life from the Loam](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Life+from+the+Loam) and upwards of 30 lands including [Maze of Ith](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maze+of+Ith), The Tabernacle of Pendrell Vale, [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) of course, [Dark Depths](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Depths), and [Thespian's Stage](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thespian%27s+Stage), one with [Exploration](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exploration) and [Manabond](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Manabond), one with [Mox Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Diamond), [Smallpox](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smallpox), [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil), and an [Entomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entomb)-powered toolbox for the likes of [Raven's Crime](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raven%27s+Crime) and [Punishing Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Punishing+Fire). 

**Shardless BUG** – This black-blue-green aggro-control deck's mana curve runs higher than BUG [Delver](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver)'s for planeswalkers [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil) and [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), eschewing most countermagic for the eponymous Shardless Agent to cascade into [Ancestral Vision](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancestral+Vision) set up by [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm). 

**BUG Control** – One step further, then, is this deck which drops all creatures and [Ancestral Vision](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancestral+Vision) in favor of more counterspells, more planeswalkers, [Pernicious Deed](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pernicious+Deed), [Innocent Blood](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Innocent+Blood), and [Standstill](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Standstill). 

**Enchantress** – [Argothian Enchantress](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Argothian+Enchantress) and [Enchantress's Presence](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Enchantress%27s+Presence), along with [Wild Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Growth) and [Utopia Sprawl](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Utopia+Sprawl), build the basis for this old-time favorite, which aims to build an impenetrable fort with [Sterling Grove](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sterling+Grove), [Solitary Confinement](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Solitary+Confinement), or [Moat](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Moat), then win via [Sigil of the Empty Throne](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sigil+of+the+Empty+Throne) or [Rest in Peace](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rest+in+Peace)/[Helm of Obedience](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Helm+of+Obedience). 

**Burn** – [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide), [Chain Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chain+Lightning), [Flame Rift](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flame+Rift), [Fireblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fireblast), [Price of Progress](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Price+of+Progress), [Sulfuric Vortex](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sulfuric+Vortex), and its ilk make for one very straightforward strategy. 

**UR Delver** – The many flavors of [Delver](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver)s also include this two-color version which usually employs [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer), sometimes [Young Pyromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Young+Pyromancer) and [True-Name Nemesis](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=True-Name+Nemesis) and more burn than its red-blue-green counterpart. 

**Affinity** – Normally, [Arcbound Ravager](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcbound+Ravager) and [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) deliver the beats only in Modern but sometimes the robots rear their heads in Legacy too, and here they can even draw upon the power of [Seat of the Synod](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seat+of+the+Synod) and Co. 

**Infect** – In Legacy, [Glistener Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glistener+Elf), [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus), and [Blighted Agent](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blighted+Agent) can be boosted not just with the more modern cards [Might of Old Krosa](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Might+of+Old+Krosa) and [Vines of Vastwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vines+of+Vastwood) but also, for example, with [Invigorate](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Invigorate) and [Berserk](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Berserk). 

**MUD** – Often completely colorless, the deck starts with [City of Traitors](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=City+of+Traitors) or [Ancient Tomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Tomb), drops [Chalice of the Void](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chalice+of+the+Void) or [Trinisphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Trinisphere) for early defense, then ramps into unfair stuff via [Grim Monolith](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Monolith) or [Metalworker](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Metalworker), finishing with [Wurmcoil Engine](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wurmcoil+Engine), [Sundering Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sundering+Titan), or [Kuldotha Forgemaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kuldotha+Forgemaster) for [Blightsteel Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightsteel+Colossus). 

And that's a wrap. The 32 most likely candidates to be played this weekend are listed above, but still there's more. Some players may choose the combo deck named for its centerpiece [Aluren](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aluren), for example, some may dust off what is considered to be an out-dated archetype in Stax, and some may do weird stuff with [Rest in Peace](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rest+in+Peace)/[Energy Field](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Energy+Field)/[Helm of Obedience](https://web.archive.org/web/20140215000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Helm+of+Obedience). Moreover, in Legacy even a shift of a few cards, what elsewhere would be a minor variation, can create a new decktype, and of course there's always the possibility for major innovation as well. 

In any case, it's going to be an interesting tournament to watch!

  
  

* * *

  
  

*   ### Saturday, 10:55 a.m. – Grand Prix Trial Winners

    *by Tobi Henke*
  

As is tradition, the weekend started on Friday with a number of Grand Prix Trials, the last chance for players to earn two byes for the Grand Prix and the first chance for us to get an impression of how the field may look like at this Legacy event. The following is a sampling of 24 decklists whose pilots went 5-0 to win one of these Trial tournaments.
